# Economics Form 4, Form Four Economics: authored answers for review

Every answer fixed by a person, not computed. Part 1: the tables, conventions and rules that computed answers come from.
Part 2: every authored question template, with all its data (keys, statements, pairs, choices and feedback) and sources.
Regenerate with `npm run review:f4-economics`. Item IDs (TR-…) match `docs/teacher-review.md`.

## Part 1

Rules the computed answers use are listed in the subject’s library tests; every authored answer is in Part 2.

### `n4-1-terms` · Lesson 1 · multiple choice, level 1

Prompt: {X.q}

Table `X` (one row is picked each time):

| q | r | w | e | y |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which term means ownership by one person, even when employees are hired? | sole ownership | unlimited liability · direct control · accounting profit | unlimited liability concerns personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model, rather than the concept asked for. · direct control concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval, rather than the concept asked for. · accounting profit concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period, rather than the concept asked for. | Ownership by one person, even when employees are hired. |
| Which term means personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model? | unlimited liability | sole ownership · direct control · accounting profit | sole ownership concerns ownership by one person, even when employees are hired, rather than the concept asked for. · direct control concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval, rather than the concept asked for. · accounting profit concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period, rather than the concept asked for. | Personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model. |
| Which term means the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval? | direct control | sole ownership · unlimited liability · accounting profit | sole ownership concerns ownership by one person, even when employees are hired, rather than the concept asked for. · unlimited liability concerns personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model, rather than the concept asked for. · accounting profit concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period, rather than the concept asked for. | The owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. |

This case instead concerns personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model. · That concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. | Bih changes the service list without consulting a partner.
Which concept best explains the highlighted feature? | direct control | sole ownership · unlimited liability · accounting profit | That concerns ownership by one person, even when employees are hired. This case instead concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. · That concerns personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model. This case instead concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. · That concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period. This case instead concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. | Quick decisions remain exposed to mistakes and limited expertise. |
| The stall deducts its costs from its sales revenue.
Which concept best explains the highlighted feature? | accounting profit | sole ownership · unlimited liability · direct control | That concerns ownership by one person, even when employees are hired. This case instead concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period. · That concerns personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model. This case instead concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period. · That concerns the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. This case instead concerns sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period. | Costs must be deducted before interpreting the remainder as profit. |

### `n4-1-repair` · Lesson 1 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect economic claim.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ sole ownership: ownership by one person, even when employees are hired.
- ✅ unlimited liability: personal responsibility that can extend beyond the owner’s business investment in the ordinary model.
- ✅ direct control: the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval.
- ✅ accounting profit: sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period.
- ❌ Hiring a worker automatically creates shared ownership.  
  _↳ A wage contract is different from owning a stake in the business._
- ❌ A sole proprietor can never be exposed beyond invested cash.  
  _↳ The ordinary model does not separate the proprietor’s liability as a limited company does._
- ❌ Direct control guarantees that every decision is correct.  
  _↳ Quick decisions remain exposed to mistakes and limited expertise._
- ❌ All sales revenue is automatically profit.  
  _↳ Costs must be deducted before interpreting the remainder as profit._


Three generated variants:

> Find the incorrect economic claim.

- ✅ Hiring a worker automatically creates shared ownership.  
  _↳ explanation: A wage contract is different from owning a stake in the business._
- ◻️ direct control: the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval.  
  _↳ explanation: This is correct: the owner’s ability to make decisions without co-owner approval. Look for a statement that contradicts the lesson’s distinction._
- ◻️ sole ownership: ownership by one person, even when employees are hired.  
  _↳ explanation: This is correct: ownership by one person, even when employees are hired. Look for a statement that contradicts the lesson’s distinction._
- ◻️ accounting profit: sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period.  
  _↳ explanation: This is correct: sales revenue less the relevant recorded costs for the period. Look for a statement that contradicts the lesson’s distinction._

### `n4-19-repair` · Lesson 19 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect economic claim.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ input access: proximity or connection to the resources an activity needs.
- ✅ market access: the ability to reach customers effectively.
- ✅ labour suitability: availability of the skills and workforce needed for the activity.
- ✅ infrastructure reliability: dependability of essential services and connections.
- ❌ Every business must locate beside the same raw material.  
  _↳ Activities require different inputs, so their location priorities differ._
- ❌ Nearness to customers cannot affect demand or delivery costs.  
  _↳ Accessibility can affect both service demand and distribution costs._
- ❌ Any available worker automatically has every specialist skill.  
  _↳ The type and capability of labour matter as well as its number._

### `n4-20-repair` · Lesson 20 · spot the error, level 3

Prompt: Find the incorrect economic claim.

Shows 3 correct and 1 wrong statement(s), drawn from:

- ✅ localisation: concentration of related industrial activities in an area.
- ✅ cluster benefit: an advantage from nearby related firms, skills or suppliers.
- ✅ location incentive: a policy measure intended to attract or support activity in a place.
- ✅ complete site comparison: weighing relevant costs and constraints rather than a single attractive feature.
- ❌ Localisation means only one firm choosing any isolated site.  
  _↳ The term concerns a concentration of activities, not just an individual location._
- ❌ Clustering always has advantages and never congestion costs.  
  _↳ Benefits must be weighed against crowding and competing resource demands._
- ❌ An incentive alone proves every site condition is suitable.  
  _↳ Service, demand and operating constraints remain even if one cost is reduced._
- ❌ The lowest rent is always the lowest total cost and best feasible site.  
  _↳ Other costs and essential conditions can overturn a rent-only ranking._
